This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am operating as an independent scientific reviewer ("Reviewer") for the `schema_semantic_constrained_ie` project. My role is to audit experiment results, verify data consistency between registry/logs/paper, check GPU resource compliance, and flag issues to the Director via `reviewer_post_to_director`. I respond to system notifications about worker completions and experiment status changes (on_complete, on_failed). I do NOT execute experiments or modify files — only read and report.

   The project is building OntoCoDec, a two-layer constrained decoding system (grammar + semantic) that compiles IE ontology schemas into token-level constraints, targeting EMNLP 2026 ARR submission (May 25). Current phase: completing the DocRED LoRA composability matrix (2 models × 2 datasets × 2 training regimes × 2 decoding modes) and expanding to a third model (Mistral).

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **OntoCoDec**: Two-layer constrained decoding (grammar layer eliminates RV/ET violations, semantic layer eliminates DR violations)
   - **Composability Matrix**: 2×2 design per model (Zero-shot vs LoRA Fine-tuned) × (Unconstrained vs Constrained S1 decoding)
   - **Violation Types**: DR (domain/range), RV (relation vocabulary), ET (entity type), CF (cross-field consistency)
   - **Key Claim (Claim 3)**: Fine-tuning and constrained decoding are complementary — FT improves F1, constraints ensure compliance at the long tail
   - **Models**: Qwen3-8B, Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct, Mistral (newly added as third model)
   - **Datasets**: SciERC (551 test docs), DocRED (998 dev docs), TACRED (being analyzed)
   - **LoRA Fine-tuning**: rank=16, alpha=32, all-linear targets, 3 epochs, effective batch 16
   - **Server**: westd-16980 with 2× NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell (98GB each), cuda:0 + cuda:1
   - **submit_training_job**: Wrapper that manages CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES, tmux sessions, exit code tracking, and on_complete/on_failed hooks

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **Registry (registry.yaml)**: Central experiment tracking. Key experiments reviewed:
     - `w5_llama_docred_lora_unconstrained_eval`: DONE/success, metrics: DR=0.49%, R-F1=43.72, E-F1=83.93, parse_fail=2.3%
     - `w5_qwen_docred_lora_unconstrained_eval`: DONE/success, metrics: DR=0.76%, R-F1=40.86, E-F1=82.15, parse_fail=7.11%, also has excl_fail variants
     - `w5_llama_docred_lora_constrained_eval`: RUNNING on cuda:0 (PID 317464/317469), 596/998 at last check
     - `w5_qwen_docred_lora_constrained_eval`: RUNNING on cuda:1 (PID 321568), 197/998 at last check
     - `w6_mistral_scierc_unconstrained`, `w6_mistral_scierc_constrained`, `w6_mistral_docred_unconstrained`: PREPARING (newly registered)
   
   - **`/root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/paper/section5_composability.tex`**: Composability table with SciERC data. Verified all numbers match registry:
     - Qwen LoRA: DR 1.95%→0.00%, R-F1 43.86→43.77 (Δ-0.09pp)
     - Llama LoRA: DR 1.11%→0.09%, R-F1 45.45→45.47 (Δ+0.02pp)
     - Table needs DocRED rows added once constrained evals complete
   
   - **`/root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/paper/main.tex`**: Contains Limitations section stating "DocRED fine-tuning is not reported" — now outdated, needs updating after constrained evals complete
   
   - **`/root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/scripts/run_docred_lora_eval.sh`**: Convenience eval script supporting 4 modes (qwen/llama × unconstrained/constrained). GPU assignments: Qwen→cuda:1, Llama→cuda:0. Created by dryrun prep worker.
   
   - **Merged Checkpoints verified**:
     - `/root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints/llama_lora_docred_merged/` (~16GB, 4 shards)
     - `/root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints/qwen_lora_docred_merged/` (~16GB, 4 shards)
   
   - **ROADMAP.md**: Last updated 2026-05-15 18:13 UTC (stale). Shows SciERC 2×2 complete for both models, DocRED marked as in-progress. Deadline: May 19 internal draft → May 25 EMNLP ARR submission.

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **Llama eval timeout (CRITICAL→resolved)**: 
     - `timeout 14400` (4h) in wrapper was insufficient for 998 DocRED samples (~4h10min needed)
     - Process killed at 775/998 with EXIT_CODE=143 (SIGTERM)
     - I flagged this as WARNING before it happened; Agent resubmitted with `--resume`
     - Eventual fix: output completed to 998/998, registry reclassified from failed→success
   
   - **Registry tracking gap**: 
     - Resume process (PID 312930) ran outside submit_training_job wrapper
     - on_complete hook didn't fire; repeated false on_failed notifications
     - Fix: Agent manually verified 998/998 completion and updated registry
     - Metrics initially missing, later computed and added
   
   - **Duplicate constrained eval processes (CRITICAL→resolved)**:
     - Two identical processes (PID 316355 + 316888) both writing to same output file on cuda:0
     - Race condition: two workers each launched a process independently
     - Both killed, corrupted output deleted, clean resubmission via submit_training_job (PID 317464)
     - Root cause: registry spec was incomplete (no PID/command), so second worker couldn't detect existing process

   - **Qwen high parse failure rate (WARNING, ongoing)**:
     - 7.1% (71/998) vs Llama 2.3% (23/998) on DocRED
     - SciERC had 0% for both models
     - FAIL samples (E=0, R=0, 63.1s timeout) systematically depress recall metrics
     - Agent added `excl_fail` metric variants for transparency

5. Problem Solving:
   - Identified timeout risk before it happened (Llama 14400s timeout vs ~4h10m needed)
   - Caught duplicate process race condition that would have corrupted output data
   - Verified all paper table numbers against registry data (section5_composability.tex)
   - Tracked GPU utilization compliance throughout (both GPUs within project allocation)
   - Flagged cuda:1 idle periods after Qwen unconstrained eval completed
   - Noted Limitations section needs updating once DocRED results are complete
   - Identified parse failure rate discrepancy between models as a reporting concern

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text

Let me also check: the earlier query returned 6 PREPARING results, but with limit=3 I only got the 3 Mistral ones. The other 3 were CF fallback experiments (killed) and the SFT data gen (success but still PREPARING status - weird).


So the full set of PREPARING experiments includes those three Mistral runs with missing specs, plus three legacy experiments from earlier waves that are either killed or in an odd state.

The real issue is that the W6 Mistral experiments are completely underspecified — they're missing everything from hypothesis down to GPU assignment. That's a blocker. And the model version itself is outdated; Mistral has released newer versions since May 2024, so I should check what's available now and whether the extraction script even supports Mistral properly.
```

The `_chat_template_kwargs` function only has a special case for 'qwen' (disabling thinking). Mistral should work with the default `{}` return. This seems fine.


Now I'm preparing to report to the Director on several critical issues: the ROADMAP is significantly outdated and missing recent completions like DocRED LoRA training and Mistral W6 registration, the three W6 Mistral experiments all lack required specification fields (hypothesis, command, server, data_path, model_name, seed, gpu) which triggers a warning per the audit checklist, and I need to verify the Mistral model version being used is appropriate.

The Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3 from May 2024 is technically outdated by audit standards, but it's actually a fair choice since the other baseline models (Qwen3-8B and Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct) are similarly established rather than cutting-edge, and for constrained decoding research, model diversity matters more than recency. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. validation in \\\\S\\\\ref{sec:cross-model} establishes that\\nOntoCoDec generalizes across model families in the zero-shot regime.\\nA complementary question is whether the same constraints remain family.\\n\\n\\\\begin{table}[t]\\n\\\\centering\\n\\\\small\\n\\\\setlength{\\\\tabcolsep}{3.4pt}\\n\\\\resizebox{\\\\columnwidth}{!}{%\\n\\\\begin{tabular}{@{}lll rr rr@{}}\\n\\\\toprule\\n& & & \\\\\\\\\\n\\\\bottomrule\\n\\\\end{tabular}}%\\n\\\\caption{Composability with fine-tuning on SciERC (551 documents).\\nU: unconstrained; S1: full two-layer decoding.\\nLoRA $r{=}16$, RV and ET are 0\\\\,\\\\% in every constrained row and are\\nomitted for space.}\\n\\\\label{tab:composability}\\n\\\\end{table}\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Fine-tuning does not eliminate ontology violations.}\\nLoRA fine-tuning lifts relation F1 from 8.23 to 43.86 (Qwen) and from\\n2.68 to 45.45 (Llama)---a 35--43\\\\,pp gain that brings both models well\\nabove the zero-shot regime.\\n% w5_lora_scierc_unconstrained_eval: R-F1=0.4386; w5_llama_lora_scierc_unconstrained_eval: R-F1=0.4545\\nYet residual domain/range violations persist at 1.95\\\\,\\\\% (Qwen) and\\n1.11\\\\,\\\\% (Llama), and per-relation analysis shows tight-constraint\\nrelations remain problematic: Evaluate-for retains 13.1\\\\,\\\\% DR for the\\nfine-tuned Qwen model despite achieving zero on six of the other seven\\nrelations.\\n% w5_lora_scierc_unconstrained_eval.notes: Evaluate-for DR=13.13%\\nThis pattern matches the tightness-driven mechanism of\\n\\\\S\\\\ref{sec:method}: relations whose valid type pairs cover a small\\nfraction of $|\\\\mathcal{T}|^2$ resist coverage even at the data\\ndistribution level, because the long tail of contexts is unlikely to\\nbe exercised during fine-tuning.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Constraints compose with fine-tuning at no F1 cost.}\\nApplying full S1 decoding on top of the fine-tuned models eliminates\\nresidual DR almost entirely (Qwen 1.95\\\\%\\\\,$\\\\to$\\\\,0.00\\\\%, Llama\\n1.11\\\\%\\\\,$\\\\to$\\\\,0.09\\\\%) while relation F1 changes by less than 0.1\\\\,pp\\n($-$0.09\\\\,pp Qwen, $+$0.02\\\\,pp Llama) and entity F1 is identical to\\nthe fine-tuned unconstrained baseline in both cases.\\n% w5_lora_scierc_constrained_eval: DR=0, R-F1=0.4377, E-F1=0.6705 (vs U: 0.4386, 0.6705)\\n% w5_llama_lora_scierc_constrained_eval: DR=0.0009, R-F1=0.4547, E-F1=0.6776 (vs U: 0.4545, 0.6776)\\nThe fine-tuned model has already moved its output distribution away\\nfrom gross violations, so the constraint mask only intervenes on the\\nsmall residual mass; the cost of that intervention is negligible.\\nThe CF rate at the fine-tuned regime is also markedly lower than at\\nzero-shot (1.18\\\\,\\\\% Qwen, 0.46\\\\,\\\\% Llama vs.\\\\ 9.90\\\\,\\\\% and 6.56\\\\,\\\\%),\\nconsistent with our analysis that CF reflects semantic ambiguity in\\nborderline assignments: fine-tuned models commit to more confident\\ntype predictions, leaving fewer borderline cases for the per-relation\\nmask to flip.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Implication.}\\nFine-tuning and decoding-time enforcement are complementary, not\\nsubstitutable.\\nFine-tuning is the primary lever for extraction \\\\emph{quality} (F1),\\nwhile constrained decoding is the primary lever for ontology\\n\\\\emph{compliance} at the long tail.\\nA practitioner who fine-tunes for F1 still needs constraint\\nenforcement to deliver per-instance type validity, because the\\nrelations with tightest constraints---those most important to enforce\\nin safety-critical settings---are precisely the ones fine-tuning is\\nleast likely to cover from data alone.\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"% Appendix B — Extended Discussion\\n% Contains discussion paragraphs moved from the main text during compression.\\n\\n\\\\section{Extended Discussion}\\\\label{app:discussion}\\n\\n%%% Paragraph 1: Low F1 Practical Significance %%%\\n% exp_ids: phase2_scierc_full_s1 (R-F1=8.36%), phase2_docred_full_s1 (R-F1=8.23%),\\n%          phase0_scierc_unconstrained (DR=15.37%), phase2_docred_unconstrained (DR=18.46%)\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Practical Significance of Zero-Shot F1.}\\nOur reported relation F1 of approximately 8\\\\% on both SciERC and DocRED reflects the inherent difficulty of zero-shot closed IE, where the model receives no task-specific training examples.\\nFor reference, supervised state-of-the-art systems on SciERC achieve approximately 49--53\\\\% relation F1 with full training data~\\\\citep{zhong2021frustratingly,ye2022packed}.\\nConstraint enforcement operates largely independently of the extraction model's intrinsic capability: it restricts the output space to ontologically valid triples regardless of the underlying model's accuracy.\\nWhen applied to fine-tuned models that reach competitive F1 levels, the same constraints remain necessary---Phase~0 analysis confirms that even well-prompted models exhibit non-trivial violation rates (15.37\\\\% DR on SciERC, 18.46\\\\% on DocRED).\\n% exp_ids: phase0_scierc_unconstrained, phase2_docred_unconstrained\\nIn our evaluation, F1 serves as a \\\\emph{control variable} verifying that constraint enforcement does not degrade extraction quality, not as an optimization target.\\nThe contribution of this work is the constraint enforcement method itself; the zero-shot setting isolates its effect from confounds introduced by task-specific fine-tuning.\\n\\n%%% Paragraph 4: Decoding-Time vs. Post-Hoc Enforcement %%%\\n% exp_ids: posthoc_filtering_baseline, phase2_scierc_full_s1 (R-F1=8.36%),\\n%          phase2_docred_full_s1 (R-F1=8.23%, ET=0%)\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Decoding-Time vs.\\\\ Post-Hoc Enforcement.}\\nPost-hoc filtering---generating triples without constraints, then removing schema-violating predictions---yields higher precision and consequently higher F1 than OntoCoDec on both datasets (SciERC 8.63 vs.\\\\ 8.36; DocRED 9.61 vs.\\\\ 8.23).\\n% posthoc: SciERC F1=8.63, DocRED F1=9.61; OntoCoDec: SciERC R-F1=8.36, DocRED R-F1=8.23\\nHowever, this precision gain comes at substantial cost.\\nOn DocRED, post-hoc filtering discards 48.1\\\\% of all predicted triples, including 127 true positives (11.2\\\\% of correct predictions), reducing recall from 9.46\\\\% to 8.40\\\\%.\\n% posthoc_docred: rejection=48.1%, tp_lost=127/1137, R: 9.46→8.40\\nEntity type violations remain unresolved (ET\\\\,=\\\\,2.47\\\\% vs.\\\\ OntoCoDec's 0\\\\%), as filtering targets only domain/range and vocabulary violations.\\n% posthoc_docred: ET_residual=2.47%; phase2_docred_full_s1: ET=0%\\nOntoCoDec's decoding-time approach avoids this information loss by guiding generation toward compliant outputs rather than discarding non-compliant ones, preserving both recall and entity type validity.\\nThe two strategies are complementary: post-hoc filtering can serve as an additional safety net for residual violations that decoding-time enforcement does not fully resolve.\\n\\n%%% CF--DR Re-routing analytical finding (W1 pilot) %%%\\n\\\\paragraph{CF--DR Re-routing: Why the Current Design Is Near-Optimal.}\\nA natural question is whether the elevated CF rate under full S1 can be\\nreduced by triggering grammar-first fallback whenever the semantic layer\\nwould assign an entity a type inconsistent with a previously registered\\nassignment for the same entity.\\nWe piloted this variant on SciERC with Qwen3-8B and observed that CF drops\\nsharply from 9.90\\\\,\\\\% to 0.04\\\\,\\\\% ($-$9.86\\\\,pp), but DR \\\\emph{rises} from\\n0.93\\\\,\\\\% to 14.78\\\\,\\\\% ($+$13.85\\\\,pp), nearly matching the unconstrained baseline.\\n% w1_cf_fallback_scierc_pilot: DR=0.1478, CF=0.0004; phase2_scierc_full_s1: DR=0.0093, CF=0.099\\nThe combined violation budget $(\\\\text{DR}+\\\\text{CF})$ therefore grows from\\n10.83\\\\,\\\\% to 14.82\\\\,\\\\%, a net increase of 3.99\\\\,pp; relation F1 is essentially\\nunchanged ($-$0.13\\\\,pp, 8.36\\\\,$\\\\to$\\\\,8.23), and entity F1 is identical to baseline.\\n% R-F1 8.36→8.23 (-0.13pp), E-F1 30.98 unchanged\\nThe fallback mechanism fires on 36.6\\\\,\\\\% of mask calls overall, with a\\nCF-registry-specific trigger rate of 4.5\\\\,\\\\% (17{,}024 mask calls; 6{,}237\\nfallback events).\\n% w1_cf_fallback_scierc_pilot: grammar_first_fallback_rate=0.3664, cf_registry_fallback_rate=0.0448\\nThese numbers reveal a fundamental tension: the two violation types\\ndraw from the same pool of constrained tokens.\\nTriggering fallback to satisfy cross-entity coherence releases the\\ndomain/range guarantee for the token in question, re-routing the\\nviolation rather than eliminating it.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Schema ambiguity dominates residual CF.}\\nWe further decomposed the 245~CF-affected entities by conflict pattern and\\nfound that 60 (24.5\\\\,\\\\%) involve \\\\textsc{Generic}$\\\\leftrightarrow$\\\\textsc{Other\\\\-Sci\\\\-en\\\\-tific\\\\-Term},\\nand 63.3\\\\,\\\\% in aggregate trace to entity types that appear in the\\ndomain/range of multiple relations with cross-cutting type intersections\\n(e.g., \\\\textsc{Method}$\\\\cap$\\\\textsc{Task}, \\\\textsc{Material}$\\\\cap$\\\\textsc{Generic}).\\n% w1_cf_fallback_scierc_pilot.notes: dominant pattern Generic↔OtherScientificTerm 60/245\\nThese conflicts reflect genuine schema-level ambiguity---multiple\\nrelation-specific type assignments are simultaneously ontologically valid---rather than enforcement-side failures.\\nPer-relation independent enforcement is thus the appropriate locus of\\ncontrol: globalizing the registry would either re-route violations\\n(as shown above) or require offline disambiguation outside the\\ngeneration loop.\\nWe conclude that the current two-layer design occupies a near-Pareto-optimal\\nposition on the (DR, CF, F1) frontier: any attempt to reduce CF through\\nlocal intervention shifts cost to DR with no net violation reduction.\\nGenuine CF resolution requires global entity-level type consensus,\\nwhich we identify as future work (\\\\S~Limitations).\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}

phase2_scierc_full_s1: DR=0.0093, CF=0.099\\nThe combined violation budget $(\\\\text{DR}+\\\\text{CF})$ therefore grows from\\n10.83\\\\,\\\\% to 14.82\\\\,\\\\%, a net increase of 3.99\\\\,pp; relation F1 is essentially\\nunchanged ($-$0.13\\\\,pp, 8.36\\\\,$\\\\to$\\\\,8.23), and entity F1 is identical to baseline.\\n% R-F1 8.36→8.23 (-0.13pp), E-F1 30.98 unchanged\\nThe fallback mechanism fires on 36.6\\\\,\\\\% of mask calls overall, with a\\nCF-registry-specific trigger rate of 4.5\\\\,\\\\% (17{,}024 mask calls; 6{,}237\\nfallback events).\\n% w1_cf_fallback_scierc_pilot: grammar_first_fallback_rate=0.3664, cf_registry_fallback_rate=0.0448\\nThese numbers reveal a fundamental tension: the two violation types\\ndraw from the same pool of constrained tokens.\\nTriggering fallback to satisfy cross-entity coherence releases the\\ndomain/range guarantee for the token in question, re-routing the\\nviolation rather than eliminating it.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Schema ambiguity dominates residual CF.}\\nWe further decomposed the 245~CF-affected entities by conflict pattern and\\nfound that 60 (24.5\\\\,\\\\%) involve \\\\textsc{Generic}$\\\\leftrightarrow$\\\\textsc{Other\\\\-Sci\\\\-en\\\\-tific\\\\-Term},\\nand 63.3\\\\,\\\\% in aggregate trace to entity types that appear in the\\ndomain/range of multiple relations with cross-cutting type intersections\\n(e.g., \\\\textsc{Method}$\\\\cap$\\\\textsc{Task}, \\\\textsc{Material}$\\\\cap$\\\\textsc{Generic}).\\n% w1_cf_fallback_scierc_pilot.notes: dominant pattern Generic↔OtherScientificTerm 60/245\\nThese conflicts reflect genuine schema-level ambiguity---multiple\\nrelation-specific type assignments are simultaneously ontologically valid---rather than enforcement-side failures.\\nPer-relation independent enforcement is thus the appropriate locus of\\ncontrol: globalizing the registry would either re-route violations\\n(as shown above) or require offline disambiguation outside the\\ngeneration loop.\\nWe conclude that the current two-layer design occupies a near-Pareto-optimal\\nposition on the (DR, CF, F1) frontier: any attempt to reduce CF through\\nlocal intervention shifts cost to DR with no net violation reduction.\\nGenuine CF resolution requires global entity-level type consensus,\\nwhich we identify as future work (\\\\S~Limitations).\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"

**格式硬约束**:

- 禁止在标签外写任何寒暄 / 说明 / 确认（"好的"/"我开始写"/"写完了"等）

**必须覆盖以下 5 个 section（缺一不可，无内容写 N/A）**:

### 1. 🔴 硬约束 / 红线
  本项目特定的不可违反约束（GPU 设备号、服务器名、账号、路径限制等）。
  写本项目特定的，不要抄 CLAUDE.md 通用 L1 红线。
  下一个"你"第一眼看到的就是这里，写错了会被 Director CRITICAL。

### 2. 🖥️ 基础设施快照
  - 服务器名 + 工作目录 + Python 路径 + conda env
  - 模型路径 / 数据集路径 / wandb config / HF cache
  - 活跃进程：PID + tmux session + cron 任务 ID
  写一次就稳定，下一个"你"不用再 SSH 探查。

### 3. 📊 当前状态

  - 等待中的 Worker 回调 / Cron / Director 决策

### 4. 📋 待办（条件触发式）
  格式 "条件 X 满足 → 动作 Y"。例如:
  - "Falcon-H1R 下载完成 → sed 修 MODEL_PATH → dry-run → 提交"
  - "pilot_011 出 GC4 结果 → ≥2pp 转架构 narrative，<2pp 写 null-result"

### 5. 🎯 战略上下文
  - 项目核心结论 / 决策链（D001..）
  - 下一个"你"最容易判断错的事 / 容易踩的坑

**写作原则**：
- 第 1 节必须是项目特定的（不是通用 L1 红线）
- 第 2 节让下一轮 BOOT 后能立即开工，避免重新 SSH 探查
- 简洁 > 完整：每节 5-10 行足够，长 handoff 不等于好 handoff
